- **pilot-help**: Read-only help over the installed PILOT skills and agents; never claims a ticket or invokes another skill. No argument lists every command; a command name gives its modes, flags and examples; an agent name gives that persona's identity and duties; a flag (--multi, --resume, …) says which commands accept it; free text describing a goal returns the exact command to run, or the persona responsible for a kind of judgment. Everything is re-derived from the current skill and agent files, so it cannot drift. Use to find out what exists, what a command or flag does, or which command fits a goal. ([Jonas-Eve/pilot](https://github.com/Jonas-Eve/pilot/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/pilot-help) · ★3)

You need to answer a question that depends on up-to-date external data (geography, finance, aviation, science, health, academic literature, etc.) and do not want to hit the upstream APIs yourself. 2. - **pilot-spec**: Phase 2 of PILOT: the architect and tech lead, in dialogue, split an already-formalized level:story into dev-sized tasks (mandatory for type:feature, a judgment call for type:tech) and write each ticket's technical spec in one pass; the PM checks a type:feature split against the acceptance criteria. Also specs a standalone type:bug task, originates a standalone type:tech need or a type:bug from a raw description (--tech, --bug), records dependencies, decides wont-do, and reclaims a story at status:qa for a new round. Pair by default (the project's workflow may default to auto), --auto, --pair… ([Jonas-Eve/pilot](https://github.com/Jonas-Eve/pilot/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/pilot-spec) · ★3)
- **pilot-spec**: Phase 2 of PILOT (see .pilot/pilot-process.md): the architect and tech lead work an already-formalized level:story together, in dialogue (.pilot/pilot-link-agent-dialogue.md) rather than one handing a finished decomposition to the other — split it into dev-sized tasks (mandatory for type:feature, a judgment call for type:tech) and write each resulting ticket's technical spec, in one continuous pass, so a spec-time feasibility concern can reshape the split immediately instead of surfacing later.
